> **RESOLVED banner (2026-07-15):** `ERR-2026-003` is `RECOVERED`. The `CG-S3-ARCH-014..016` and `CG-S5-PH0-001..003` rows below were duplicated because two independent lineages both completed these tasks and both were merged into `main` without reconciliation. The operator authorized adopting **Lineage A**: `docs/architecture/14..16_*.md` are now single coherent Lineage A documents (607-item authoritative baseline), Prompt 82 (`CG-S5-PH0-003`) was re-verified against it (`docs/build-log/phase-00/PH0-82.md`), and Phase 0 build logs were consolidated onto the prompt-package-prescribed singular path `docs/build-log/phase-00/PH0-NN.md` (the plural Lineage B duplicates removed). The `VERIFIED` statuses for these seven task IDs are now reliable against the reconciled baseline. Phase 0 execution resumes at `CG-S5-PH0-004`. See `ERROR_LEDGER.md` `ERR-2026-003` and `HANDOFF.md` §1. Any historical duplicate rows retained below are evidence of the incident, not active state.

## 1. Task identity and state model

Step 2 discovery tasks use `CG-S2-DISC-<NNN>`; reconciliation tasks append `-R<n>`. Allowed states: `NOT_STARTED → READY → IN_PROGRESS → COMPLETED → VERIFIED`, plus documented exceptions.

New narrow wrapper `app.pipeline_scope_org_unit_ids` (descendant-plus-self scope, wrapping `app.org_unit_descendant_ids`, `PLT-109`) is the query-direction sibling of `COM-143`'s ancestor-plus-self `app.lead_record_scope_org_unit_ids`, not a duplicate. **One real defect found and fixed this checkpoint, in a Platform Core primitive**: `app.can_access_record` (`PLT-114`) compared `p_owner_user_id = p_auth_user_id` directly — since `app.sales_plans`/`app.sales_targets` are the first tables in this repository whose `owner_user_id` can legitimately be `NULL` (org-unit-wide targets), this produced SQL `NULL` rather than `false` for a non-owner, and `false OR null` is `NULL`, which every existing caller's `if not app.can_access_record(...)` guard treated as not-true (i.e., silently did not raise) — a NULL-owner comparison could silently *grant* access instead of denying it. Latent since `PLT-114`, never previously exercised or caught, found via a genuine `db:test` failure (a sibling-team outsider was not denied access to a team-scoped target) the first time this checkpoint's own suite exercised the null-owner path; fixed via `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION app.can_access_record` in this migration (the applied `PLT-114` file itself is never edited) — the owner comparison is now deterministically boolean and the full `OR` expression is wrapped in a defense-in-depth `coalesce(..., false)`. Also extended `app.prospects` (`COM-144`) with nullable `disqualified_at`/`archived_at` columns (additive) and superseded `app.disqualify_prospect`/`app.archive_prospect` via `CREATE OR REPLACE` to set them, needed for accurate period-scoped metric reconciliation. (2) `scripts/db-tests/master-data.sql`'s own pre-existing `app.v_active_vendor_rates` assertion broke once this checkpoint's real approved rows existed and its column assumptions changed — fixed by rewriting it to expect 0 rows for its own tenant, proactively scoped by `tenant_id` (applying the `COM-145`/`COM-148` cross-file-fragility lesson to a new class — a shared-view assertion, not an audit-log count — before it could be rediscovered a third time); Exact, versioned, explainable margin/markup calculation over a selected cost snapshot (`app.rate_selections`, `COM-149`) and selling inputs. Four disclosed boundaries: money stays PostgreSQL `numeric` end to end (never binary floating point, Prompt 150 §24) with explicit `round(..., 2)` at every money-producing step; margin rules are tenant-wide only (no per-org-unit rule in this bounded slice); FX/multi-currency conversion is out of scope (`app.calculate_margin` fails closed with `mixed_currency` unless the selling currency exactly matches the pinned cost snapshot's own currency); this is the calculation engine and its own governed override, not the Quotation Builder itself (`COM-151`, the very next capability). `app.margin_rule_versions` (a versioned, tenant-wide minimum-margin-percentage policy, `draft`→`published`→`archived`, `app.publish_margin_rule_version`'s `p_supersedes_version_id` archives the prior published rule exactly like `app.publish_sales_plan`, `COM-146` — at most one published rule per tenant, a partial unique index). `app.margin_calculations` (one deterministic calculation per `(rate_selection, sell inputs)`, pinning the exact cost snapshot and the exact rule version/minimum/rounding in effect — a later edit to either never silently reprices a past calculation; a recalculation for the same `rate_selection_id` creates a new row and marks the prior one `is_current=false`/`superseded_by_id`, mirroring `app.pipeline_outcomes`'s own chain, `COM-146`). `app.calculate_margin` (requires both `COM:Edit` and `COM:View cost`, mirroring `COM-148`'s `app.submit_costing_response`/`COM-149`'s `app.select_vendor_rate` dual gate) and `app.override_margin_threshold` (`COM:Approve`-gated, mandatory reason, only valid against an un-overridden `requires_approval` result, never changes the source figures). `app.margin_calculations_directory` masks two independent dimensions — cost/margin/markup behind `COM:View cost`, sell/discount/net-sell behind `COM:View selling price` (`COM-147`) — no new permission seeded; `app.margin_rule_versions` itself stays tenant-wide visible without masking (policy config, not a specific deal's financial figure, mirroring `app.win_loss_reasons`). **Two real defects found and fixed**: (1) `app.calculate_margin`'s own recalculation-supersede logic initially updated the prior row's `superseded_by_id` to the new row's id *before* inserting that new row — a foreign-key violation, found via a genuine `db:test` failure on the first recalculation scenario; fixed with the correct three-step order (mark prior non-current first, insert the new row, only then link `superseded_by_id`), since the new row must exist before it can be referenced and the prior row must already be non-current before the new row can satisfy the `(rate_selection_id) where is_current` partial unique index. (2) A pre-existing, latent cross-file fragility in `COM-149`'s own `commercial-rate-cost-lookup.sql` was exposed for the first time by this checkpoint's own fixture reusing the identical `Jakarta`/`Surabaya`/`FCL`/`ocean_freight` lane: that file's rate-version/rate-selection lookups carried no `tenant_id` filter, and once this checkpoint's own db-test file ran first (alphabetical order), those lookups became genuinely ambiguous across the shared disposable database, manifesting as a real tenant_admin being reported to lack authority (the fetched row actually belonged to a different tenant). Root-caused via direct data inspection (ruling out a session-state/RLS explanation first, since each db-test file is its own fresh connection) and fixed durably by scoping every affected lookup in `commercial-rate-cost-lookup.sql` to its own tenant — the same class of fix `COM-145`/`148` established for unscoped audit-log counts, applied here to unscoped entity lookups. Full service layer (`server/contracts|queries|mutations/margin*`) plus a new route (`app/(tenant)/[tenantSlug]/commercial/margin-rules/`, rule list + create form) and a Margin Calculation section on the Costing Request Detail page. **Disclosed UI scope boundary**: the margin-rule publish action is a bare inline form with no per-row error surface. `node:test` 1115/1115 (21 net new), `db:test` PASS across 40 migrations/40 db-test files (zero regression, one sibling file corrected), a real `next build` succeeded producing 21 routes (up from 20), all other gates green. Two-dimension field masking (`app.quotations_directory`/`app.quotation_lines_directory`, `COM:View selling price` for every monetary total, `COM:View cost` for the line-level cost/margin snapshot independently) — no new permission-catalogue row, reuses `COM-147`/`148`'s masking predicates directly. Six disclosed scope boundaries recorded in the migration's own header and `docs/build-log/phase-02/COM-151.md` §3.1: no canonical customer/account/address master yet (reuses `app.prospects`/`app.contacts` directly); Numbering Engine not adopted; line editing is add/remove; autosave is an explicit optimistically-concurrent save, not real-time; document generation/private preview `BLOCKED` on the still-missing asset-upload/storage pipeline (confirmed absent during this same session's prior design-system audit); revisioning/versioning and approval routing are explicitly Prompt 152/153's own scope, not built here. **Two real defects found and fixed during authoring, both via genuine `db:test` failures**: (1) `v_reasons := v_reasons || 'code'` (implicit `text[] || text` concatenation) raised a Postgres "malformed array literal" error because the operator resolved to `anyarray || anyarray` instead of `anyarray || anyelement` for an untyped string literal — fixed with explicit `array_append(v_reasons, 'code')` throughout `app.get_quotation_submission_readiness`; (2) this checkpoint's own `scripts/db-tests/commercial-quotation-builder.sql` picked "the first quotation for this customer" via `order by created_at asc limit 1`, non-deterministic when two quotations are created inside the same `do $$ ... $$` block (Postgres freezes `now()` for the whole transaction) — fixed by ordering on the guaranteed-distinct, allocation-ordered `quote_number` instead. Full service layer (`server/contracts|queries|mutations/quotation*`, 30 net new unit tests) plus new routes (`app/(tenant)/[tenantSlug]/commercial/quotations/` list and `.../quotations/[quotationId]/` builder — header, lines table with per-line remove, add-line form sourcing from the opportunity's own current margin calculations, terms/contact form, submission-readiness panel with submit/clone actions); the Opportunity Detail page (`COM-147`) gained a "Quotations" section + `CreateQuotationForm`, mirroring `COM-148`'s own `RequestCostingForm` pattern. `node:test` 1158/1158 (30 net new), `db:test` PASS across 41 migrations/41 db-test files (zero regression), a real `next build` succeeded producing 23 routes (up from 21). **One disclosed, pre-existing, unfixed tool false positive**: `pnpm run git:check-paths` flags this checkpoint's own brand-new migration file as a forbidden "applied migration edit" — its regex matches any changed path under `supabase/migrations/`, not only genuine edits to an already-merged file; verified this checkpoint that the identical false positive fires against `COM-150`'s own historical commit run through the same checker, so every prior Commercial-phase migration commit has triggered it — not new here, and the tool itself is outside this task's allowed-file scope to fix. Widens `COM-151`'s `app.quotations` with a real root/version identity (`root_quotation_id`, monotonic `version_number`, `is_current`, `superseded_by_id`, `revision_reason`) via `ALTER TABLE` -- never edits `COM-151`'s own migration file (`AGENTS.md`). Database-enforced sequence/uniqueness: `unique(root_quotation_id, version_number)` plus a partial-unique index guaranteeing exactly one `is_current=true` row per root, the same technique `app.margin_calculations_current_unique` (`COM-150`) established. `app.create_quotation_revision(p_source_quotation_id, p_reason, ...)` serves both Prompt 152's main flow ("creates a new draft revision from the latest eligible quote") and alternative flow ("an older version is restored as a new latest draft") identically -- `p_source_quotation_id` may be the current version or any historical version of the same root; both cases copy the source's header/lines into a brand-new version and supersede whichever row was current beforehand. Gated by `COM:Edit` + record access + a mandatory non-empty reason. Concurrency safety via real row-level locking (`SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` on the root's current-version row, not merely optimistic concurrency) -- a genuinely concurrent second call blocks until the first commits, then proceeds correctly against the fresh current row rather than racing or corrupting state. Six `COM-151` functions (`add_quotation_line`/`remove_quotation_line`/`update_quotation_terms`/`submit_quotation`/`create_quotation_draft`/`clone_quotation`) plus `app.quotations_directory` widened via `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION`/`VIEW` with identical signatures (the same technique `COM-149` used on `app.can_access_record` and `COM-132` used on three `PLT-131` functions) -- the four mutation functions now additionally require `is_current=true`, closing a real gap: without this widening, a superseded historical version whose own `status` column still literally read `draft` (revised before ever being submitted) would have remained directly editable. **Five disclosed scope boundaries** (migration header and `docs/build-log/phase-02/COM-152.md` §3.1): no "issued"/"accepted" status values invented ahead of real evidence (`COM-153` Quotation Approval / `COM-154` Customer Acceptance have not run) -- the lock rule is bounded to `is_current` + `status <> 'cancelled'`, with the exact extension point named for those future capabilities to widen; "supersede" is the internal side-effect of `create_quotation_revision`, never a separate user-invoked action; "compare" is computed in TypeScript (`server/contracts/quotation/quotation-diff.ts`, pure and unit-tested -- two already field-masked directory reads already carry every access decision the diff needs, no third SQL function has a real rule left to enforce); RPD-022 (Supreme Admin absolute CRUD) is disclosed, not newly implemented; legacy data migration is not applicable (greenfield, no live environment). Full service layer plus Quotation Detail page additions: a Version column, line/term/submit editing gated on `status===draft && isCurrent` (restated at the UI layer, the real gate is server-side), `VersionHistory` (every version of the root with View/Compare links), `RevisionForm` (mandatory reason, label switches between "Create new revision"/"Restore as new draft"), `ComparisonPanel` (renders a computed diff via `?compareWith=<versionId>`). **No defect found in the migration itself** -- two real test-authoring mistakes were found and fixed before the db-test suite was declared green: (1) the initial setup used the tenant_admin identity (no `COM:Create`/`COM:Approve`) to create/publish the margin rule, fixed by using the rep instead; (2) an incorrect assertion expected the freshly-revised version 2 to be "not yet ready" when it had in fact correctly inherited a satisfied contact/line state from version 1, fixed to expect `ready=true`. `node:test` 1172/1172 (14 net new), `db:test` PASS across 42 migrations/42 db-test files (zero regression, including `COM-151`'s own test file running unmodified against the widened schema), a real `next build` succeeded producing 23 routes (unchanged count). **One disclosed, pre-existing, unfixed tool false positive** (same as `COM-151`'s own disclosure): `pnpm run git:check-paths` flags this checkpoint's own brand-new migration file as a forbidden "applied migration edit." New `app.quotation_approval_rules` (a versioned, tenant-wide threshold policy -- `min_margin_pct`/`max_discount_pct`/`min_value_amount`, each optional but at least one required -- mirrors `app.margin_rule_versions`, `COM-150`, line for line: draft/published/archived, `supersedes_version_id`, exactly one published row per tenant via a partial unique index). Widens `app.quotations` (`ALTER TABLE`, never edits `COM-151`/`152`'s own migration files) with a second axis independent of submission `status`: `approval_status` (not_required/pending/approved/rejected), `approval_request_id`, `approval_rule_version_id`, `approval_required_reasons` (reason codes only, never a dollar figure -- the same masking-independent-safety discipline `app.get_quotation_submission_readiness`, `COM-151`, already established). `app.evaluate_quotation_approval_requirement` is the one deterministic threshold decision (margin via `min(quotation_lines.margin_pct_snapshot)`, discount via the header's own `discount_amount/subtotal_amount`, value via `total_amount` directly). `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION app.submit_quotation` (identical signature, widened) now resolves routing inside the same transaction as the draft->submitted move: no published rule crossed -> auto-approved, no request ever created; a threshold crossed -> looks up the tenant's published `config_type_code='approval'` routing definition (published via the unmodified generic `PLT-121`/`123` RPCs -- no new SQL, no dedicated Commercial authoring UI, the same "ships with zero authoring UI" precedent `PLT-123` itself set) and opens a real routed request via `app.request_approval`, failing closed (`approval_definition_not_configured`) if none is published rather than silently letting the quotation through. New `app.decide_quotation_approval_step` is the one domain-specific sync wrapper this checkpoint adds over the engine -- it validates the bound request's `entity_type='quotation'`, delegates the real decision to the unmodified `app.decide_approval_step` (eligibility/self-approval-denial/duplicate-decision guards all reused, not re-implemented), then syncs `app.quotations.approval_status` only once the request reaches a final state. Delegation/escalation need no such wrapper (neither changes a request's final outcome) -- the API layer calls the engine's own functions directly. "Request revision" is not a fourth decision verb -- an approver rejects with a reason, then `app.create_quotation_revision` (`COM-152`, unmodified) starts a fresh governed approval path; the new revision defaults `approval_status='not_required'` by column default, never inheriting the rejected verdict. Legacy submitted quotations are never retroactively approved -- every pre-existing row defaults to `not_required` purely because it predates this capability, no backfill performed. Token expiry is bound to the quotation's own `validity_to`, never a separately invented policy. No scheduled expiry/reminder job (`PLT-132`'s Background Job Framework has no live worker anywhere in this repository) and no real email/SMTP delivery (no provider wired anywhere) -- both disclosed, not invented; the seller relays the real, secure, returned link through their own channel. Full service layer (`server/contracts|queries|mutations/quotation-acceptance*`) plus seller-side UI (`CustomerAcceptancePanel`/`SendAcceptanceForm` on the Quotation Detail page, showing the raw link exactly once immediately after a successful send) and **the first public, unauthenticated route this repository adds beyond `/login`** -- `app/(public)/quote-decision/[token]/` (customer-safe read + accept/reject decision form). **Three real defects found and fixed during authoring, all via genuine `db:test` failures**: (1) two `plpgsql` functions whose `RETURNS TABLE` declared an output column literally named `quotation_id` produced "column reference is ambiguous" against bare `quotation_id` references inside their own bodies -- fixed by table-aliasing every such reference; (2) `gen_random_bytes`/`digest` (pgcrypto, installed in `public`) were unreachable from two `SECURITY DEFINER` functions whose `search_path` excluded `public` -- fixed by widening those two functions' `search_path` to `app, public, pg_temp` (disclosed as a real, narrower-than-`PLT-129`'s-own-precedent restriction, not a regression of the `ERR-2026-004` discipline); (3) the customer-decision function's expiry check did not handle a token already lazily flipped to `expired` by a prior read -- fixed by adding an explicit branch covering both paths uniformly. `node:test` 1216/1216 (20 net new), `db:test` PASS across 44 migrations/44 db-test files (zero regression, including `COM-150`/`151`/`152`/`153`'s own test files running unmodified against the widened schema), a real `next build` succeeded producing 26 routes (up from 25, the new `/quote-decision/[token]` route). **One disclosed, pre-existing, unfixed tool false positive** (same class as `COM-151`/`152`/`153`'s own disclosure): `pnpm run git:check-paths` flags this checkpoint's own brand-new migration file as a forbidden "applied migration edit" once staged. New `app.customer_contracts` (one row per contract/pricelist version — `root_contract_id`/`version_number` identity reusing `app.quotations`' own self-referencing-insert technique, `COM-152`; unlike a quotation's single `is_current` row, multiple versions of one root may be simultaneously `status=published` as long as their `[effective_from, effective_to)` windows do not overlap, enforced at publish time via `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` row locking — the same concurrency-safety technique `app.create_quotation_revision`, `COM-152`, already established for its own analogous invariant). New `app.customer_contract_price_components` (one priced service/lane condition per row; a unique index on the five identity dimensions structurally guarantees `app.get_effective_customer_price` resolves to at most one row). `app.create_customer_contract_draft` serves both the main flow (from an accepted+converted quotation — one contract root per source quotation, ever) and the alternative flow (a renewal/amendment from any version, copying its price components into a new future-dated draft) identically. `app.publish_customer_contract` (`COM:Approve`-gated, requires ≥1 component, rejects a date-overlapping already-published sibling) and `app.retire_customer_contract` (`COM:Approve`-gated, mandatory reason) are direct governance gates, not a second Approval Engine routing — a deliberately smaller-scope choice than `COM-153`'s own quotation approval, disclosed in the migration header. `app.get_effective_customer_price` is the deterministic, reproducible lookup Prompt 156's own objective names — exact-match on every identity dimension, raises `no_effective_price` explicitly on zero rows rather than returning an empty set silently. **`COM:View margin` newly seeded for the `COM` module** (reusing the already-real `'View margin'` enum value, the same bounded `(module, action)`-pair mechanism `COM-148` already used for `COM:View cost` — not `COM-155`'s own blocked path, since `'View margin'` already exists in the fixed enum unlike the rejected `'View billing'`); masks the five money-bearing columns on `app.customer_contract_price_components` via the already-established `COM:View selling price` instead, since no separate margin figure is computed or stored here. Full service layer (`server/contracts|queries|mutations/contract*`) plus new routes (`commercial/contracts/` list+detail with price-component management, publish/retire/renewal actions) and a `ContractCreationPanel`/`CreateContractForm` added to the Quotation Detail page (renders once accepted and converted to an account). **Three real defects found and fixed during authoring**: (1) `app.get_effective_customer_price`'s initial body checked `if v_row is null` on a `record`-typed variable after a `select into`, which does not reliably detect a zero-row match — fixed by checking the implicit `FOUND` boolean instead, the correct plpgsql idiom; (2) `app.add_customer_contract_price_component` initially gated only `COM:Edit` while returning the raw, unmasked price-component row directly, letting an actor without `View selling price` round-trip a selling price back to themselves through the RPC's own return value — caught by comparing against `app.select_vendor_rate`'s own established dual-gate precedent (`COM-149`) before any test was written, fixed by adding an explicit `has_view_selling_price` check alongside `Edit`; (3) a real `react/no-unescaped-entities` lint error in `renewal-form.tsx`'s help text — fixed. `node:test` 1265/1265 (29 net new), `db:test` PASS across 46 migrations/46 db-test files (zero regression, including `COM-144..155`'s own test files running unmodified against the widened schema). Requirement-anchor reconciliation: `docs/ai-agent-build-prompt-package/00-control/05_REQUIREMENT_COVERAGE_MATRIX.md` (read-only, untouched) confirms all 20 Phase 3 requirement-anchor items (`OPS-SHP/TMS/TRK/DOC/CST-001..004`) are `SOURCE_MAPPED`/`*_PROMPT_COMPLETE`, matching the 17 `VERIFIED` execution-index rows. **The primary result of this checkpoint is one real finding and fix**: the end-to-end record-scope isolation block (the sibling-team outsider denied on eleven cross-capability entry points against the identical fixture data in one pass, rather than the per-capability isolated checks each capability's own test already does) caught that `app.record_transaction_lineage_override` (`OPS-184`) checked only tenant-wide `OPS:Override` permission -- never whether the actor could actually access the specific Job Order/Shipment Order/handoff record named as the correction's source or target. Since `OPS:Override` is a tenant-wide grant, not record-scoped, any actor holding it anywhere in the tenant could have injected a false lineage claim about a record they otherwise have zero access to -- a Critical-class gap missed at `OPS-184`'s own authoring because that checkpoint's own db-test only ever exercised the override with an actor who legitimately owned every record involved. **Fixed via a new migration, never editing `OPS-184`'s own applied one**: `supabase/migrations/20260728180000_harden_operations_transaction_lineage_override.sql` -- `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION app.record_transaction_lineage_override` with the identical name/signature, adding an `app.can_access_record` check on whichever side(s) of the edge resolve to a real `job_order`/`shipment_order`/`job_order_handoff` row (every one of the six relation types has at least one such anchor per the table's own relation-pair check constraint); `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION` preserves the function's identity so `OPS-184`'s own grants remain unchanged, no new grant/revoke needed. Resolved within this same checkpoint rather than deferred to `CG-S8-OPS-020` (Prompt 186, Security/Financial Hardening) since it is bounded, well-understood, and directly blocks this checkpoint's own integrated test -- `OPS-186` remains free to run its own broader sweep next. No prior migration is edited -- purely additive: one new append-only edge table (`app.transaction_lineage_edges`, unique on `(tenant_id, source_type, source_id, target_type, target_id, relation_type)`) plus six new `AFTER INSERT` triggers on the six already-existing tables above, each a thin wrapper around one shared `app.capture_transaction_lineage_edge` insert-and-audit primitive -- the only way to capture "atomic lineage creation at each domain transition" without reopening `OPS-168`/`169`/`177`/`178`/`179`/`181`'s own immutable migration files. Versioned, deterministic, explainable billing-readiness evaluation per Job Order -- exact blockers when not ready, a bounded authorized-override path, and one idempotent Finance-handoff record; evidence status only, never an invoice/AR/GL entry/journal. No new permission action: evaluation/handoff reuse `OPS:Edit`, override/revoke-override reuse the already-registered `OPS:Override` (already exercised by `OPS-168`'s own `app.override_job_order_field`), matching `OPS-178`'s own "reuse an existing action" precedent. Evidence checked per non-cancelled Shipment Order belonging to the Job Order: delivery+ePOD completion (`status in ('epod', 'closed')`, the only two statuses reachable via `OPS-177`'s own completion function), the document checklist (`OPS-176`'s own missing-mandatory-items query inlined), no active operational exception (`OPS-174`'s own `status not in ('resolved', 'closed')`), and an approved actual cost (`OPS-178`'s own `is_current and status = 'approved'`). The customer billing-profile check reuses `app.job_orders.credit_snapshot` verbatim -- the exact `{outcome, checkedAt}` object `COM-164`'s own `app.prepare_job_order_handoff` already pinned from the account's most recent credit check at handoff time, the same "read the governed snapshot verbatim" discipline `OPS-179` established for `revenue_snapshot`; `outcome = 'allow'` is ready, a null snapshot (`billing_profile_missing`) or any `blocked_*` outcome (`billing_profile_not_cleared`) is not. `rule_version` is a fixed, disclosed placeholder (`1`) -- no configurable rule-engine table exists yet, the same class of disclosed simplification as `OPS-179`'s "no FX conversion" boundary. Versioned, `is_current`-exclusive; the first evaluation needs no reason, every subsequent evaluation while a current row exists requires a non-empty `reevaluation_reason`, and always resets any prior override (evidence changes do not silently preserve readiness). Override is bounded to forcing an evaluated `not_ready` row to an effective `ready` outcome, never the reverse -- `effective_status` is a **generated column** (`ready` while `is_overridden`, else the evidence-based `evaluated_status`), so the raw evidence result stays visible even while overridden; revoking restores it without ever re-evaluating evidence itself. New `supabase/migrations/20260728140000_create_operations_billing_readiness.sql`: `app.billing_readiness_evaluations` (versioned, generated `effective_status` column, partial-unique `is_current`) and `app.billing_readiness_handoffs` (append-only, idempotent on `(tenant_id, job_order_id, idempotency_key)`); `app.evaluate_billing_readiness`, `app.override_billing_readiness`, `app.revoke_billing_readiness_override`, `app.handoff_billing_readiness` (requires `effective_status = 'ready'`). RLS-scoped base tables via a join back to `app.job_orders` -- no masked directory view, since no money amount is ever exposed by this capability. | `CG-S8-OPS-014` | Basic Public Customer Tracking | Phase 3 / Operations | `VERIFIED` | Claude Code | `claude/lanjut-promp-176-sd-prom-183-oknugn` | `CG-S8-OPS-013` (`OPS-179`, `VERIFIED`) | `docs/build-log/phase-03/OPS-180.md` | 2026-07-28 | Complete — fifth task in this session's "LANJUT PROMP 176 SD PROM 183" authorized range. A minimal, fast, read-only public tracking surface: an unguessable per-shipment opaque token (`COM-154`'s own `token_hash = encode(digest(raw_token, 'sha256'), 'hex')` pattern, raw token returned exactly once at issuance, never stored) resolves to a sanitized status/timeline/ePOD-availability projection -- never a live Customer Portal. `app.lookup_public_shipment_tracking` is the one function in this schema granted `EXECUTE` to `anon` directly (a disclosed, deliberate exception to the standing `ERR-2026-004` revoke-then-explicit-re-grant convention) -- authorization is the token itself, not `auth.uid()`. It is designed to **never raise for an expected outcome** (`not_found`/`invalid`/`rate_limited`): it always returns exactly one row with a `lookup_status` column, every other column null unless `lookup_status='ok'`. This is a direct application of the defect class `OPS-175` first documented for its own dispatch failure-audit design -- a raised exception aborts the whole enclosing transaction (one PostgREST call is exactly one transaction), rolling back any insert made earlier in that same transaction, so a "log the failed attempt, then raise" pattern would silently never persist `app.tracking_lookup_attempts`. Rate limiting counts `not_found`/`invalid` attempts for a caller-supplied `client_key` (a sha256 hash of the caller's own best-effort IP, computed by the calling page/Server Action, never a raw IP stored server-side) in the trailing 15 minutes; 10 or more flips the outcome to `rate_limited`. The sanitized milestone timeline reuses `OPS-173`'s own `is_customer_visible` flag; `epod_available` is a boolean derived from `OPS-177`'s completed ePOD captures -- never the evidence itself. New `supabase/migrations/20260728130000_create_operations_public_tracking.sql`: `app.shipment_tracking_tokens` (at most one active token per shipment, partial unique index) and `app.tracking_lookup_attempts` (append-only anti-enumeration evidence); `app.issue_shipment_tracking_token` (`OPS:Edit` + record-scope gated, revokes any prior active token first), `app.revoke_shipment_tracking_token` (reason mandatory), `app.lookup_public_shipment_tracking` (described above). Full service layer (`server/contracts|queries|mutations/public-tracking.ts`) plus UI: a new "Public tracking link" issue/revoke management panel on the Shipment Order detail page (the raw link shown exactly once, mirroring `COM-154`'s own `send-acceptance-form.tsx` precedent), and a new public, unauthenticated page `app/(public)/tracking/[token]/page.tsx` (same `app/(public)/` route group as `login/`/`quote-decision/[token]/`, reading via a service-role client since no `auth.uid()` session exists there). **One architecturally significant correction made during authoring**: the lookup function originally used `raise exception` for the three non-`'ok'` outcomes, each preceded by an attempt-log insert -- testing showed the rate limiter never actually triggered after 10 failed lookups because the raised exception rolled back every prior insert in the same transaction, so the function was redesigned to the always-return `lookup_status` contract described above, cascading into a db-test and TypeScript-layer rewrite. Two smaller `plpgsql` defects also caught and fixed: an ambiguous-column reference in `issue_shipment_tracking_token`'s own `update ... where shipment_order_id = ...` (its `returns table(...)` declares an identically-named OUT column -- fixed by aliasing the update target), and untyped `null` literals in early-return branches failing to structurally match the declared `returns table(...)` types (fixed with explicit casts). `scripts/db-tests/operations-public-tracking.sql` (authority-gating, at-most-one-active-token, sanitized/filtered projection, ePOD-availability correctness, not_found/invalid/rate_limited outcomes with the attempt log surviving every one of them, revoke-reason enforcement, record-scope/cross-tenant isolation, schema-privilege defense in depth including the one deliberate `anon` grant, audit-trail reconciliation). `node:test` 1697/1697 (15 net new), `db:test` PASS across 66 db-test files (1 net new, zero regression), `npx next build` PASS (53 routes, new: `/tracking/[token]`). The pre-existing generic "Transition" section's `assigned -> dispatched` forward-affordance edge was deliberately removed from `lifecycle-transitions.ts` (a UI-only change -- the database's own `app.transition_shipment_order` still accepts that edge directly) so the UI routes dispatch exclusively through the new gated command, the same "a dedicated command supersedes the generic transition matrix" precedent `draft -> confirmed`'s own `ConfirmShipmentOrderForm` already set. **Several real issues found and fixed before the full suite passed**: (1) a db-test authoring bug (`select record_version into v_shipment`, a single-column select, corrupted a full-row variable) raising `invalid input syntax for type uuid`; (2)-(3) two real migration bugs in the failure-path `capture_audit_event` call (a stray text argument landing in a `jsonb` parameter, then a jsonb array passed where `app.redact_audit_payload` requires an object); (4) investigating those surfaced that the entire failure-audit-capture design was architecturally unreachable -- Postgres rolls back any insert made before a caught `raise exception` to the enclosing block's own implicit savepoint, true both in a caller's own `exception when others` and inside `bulk_dispatch_shipment_orders`' per-record catch -- root-cause fixed by removing the failure-path audit capture entirely (blockers are carried in the raised exception message instead) and correcting the db-test's own audit-trail assertion from an expected "3 failure events" to the architecturally-correct "0 persisted failure events"; (5) a db-test column-name bug (`outcome` vs. the real `app.audit_logs.result`); (6) two fixture-authoring bugs (vendor-resource reuse tripping `OPS-172`'s own `assignment_conflict`; a `viewer`-actor read-success assertion that fails record-scope despite same-team membership, corrected to the actual shipment owner, matching this session's own "viewer is negative-tests-only" convention). `scripts/db-tests/operations-basic-dispatch.sql` (readiness evaluation per-fixture, ready-queue listing, dispatch authority/readiness/idempotency, bulk-dispatch bounded/mixed-result, record-scope isolation, cross-tenant isolation, schema-privilege defense in depth, audit-trail reconciliation). `node:test` 1611/1611 (24 net new), `db:test` PASS across 61 db-test files (1 net new, zero regression), `npx next build` PASS (52 routes, 1 new: `/[tenantSlug]/operations/dispatch`). Status is a pure function of balance (`open`/`partial`/`paid`), computed only by the allocation functions, never accepted from a caller; collection hold is an orthogonal flag, not a status value. New `supabase/migrations/20260729100000_create_finance_accounts_receivable.sql`: `app.finance_ar_open_items` (idempotent unique on tenant/source-document-type/source-document-id; `open_amount` a generated column), `app.finance_ar_open_item_events` (append-only audit trail, idempotency-key-guarded), plus 9 functions covering authority, idempotent posting (period-aware via FIN-193's own `app.resolve_finance_period_for_date`; `invoice` requires `FIN:Edit`, `opening_balance` requires `FIN:Approve` per Prompt 196 section 24's own "approved migration/opening balance" exception), hold/release (deliberately split authority -- `FIN:Edit` places, `FIN:Approve` releases), row-locked idempotent allocation (rejects over-allocation), governed reversal (`FIN:Approve`-gated, mandatory reason), list, activity, and an internal credit-exposure aggregate. Full service layer (`server/contracts|queries|mutations/accounts-receivable.ts`) plus UI: new route `app/(tenant)/[tenantSlug]/finance/accounts-receivable/` (bounded due-date-ordered work queue with overdue highlighting, per-row hold/release actions, and a customer exposure lookup tool -- no create form, since AR is created only via a balanced source posting). `scripts/db-tests/finance-accounts-receivable.sql` (7 scenario groups: idempotent posting with full structural/authority validation, the invoice-vs-opening-balance authority split, exact-balance allocation through open->partial->paid with idempotent-replay and over-allocation rejection, governed deallocation with mandatory reason, the hold/release authority split, exposure summary and cross-tenant isolation, schema-privilege defense in depth, audit-trail reconciliation). Defined risk burn-down evidence, stop/rollback triggers, assumptions, sensitivity analysis, and recalculation rules.

### CG-S3-ARCH-016 — Step 3 Closure Verification
**Objective and outcome:** Produced a risk-ranked, dependency-depth-ordered critical path over the full WBS (`13_*.md`) and traceability matrix (`14_*.md`): defined 9 ranking dimensions (severity, likelihood, blast radius, tenant/security/finance/data exposure, dependency centrality, reversibility, detection-strength gap, uncertainty, remediation complexity), each on a reproducible 1–5 scale, combined into a Composite Risk Score (`CRS = Sev×Lik + Rad+Exp+Dep+Rev+Det+Unc+Rem`, range 8–60) with full arithmetic shown per row so ranking is independently recomputable — no duration, staffing figure, or calendar date invented anywhere. Critical path: `Phase 0 → 1 (Platform Core) → 2 (Commercial) → 3 (Operations/Portal basic) → 4 (Finance) → {5 (Advanced TMS/WMS) ‖ 6 (Procurement)} → 7 (HRIS/Ticketing) → 8 (Portal full/Loyalty) → 9 (Intelligence/Enterprise) → 15 (hardening) → 16 (RC/Go-Live) → direct GA`, matching `12_RELEASE_TRAIN.md` §9 exactly (Phase 5/6 the one genuine parallel-eligible fork). Top-ranked risks: Finance tax/legal SME gate (`FIN-195`, CRS 49), payroll SME gate (`HRT-282`, CRS 47), Supreme Admin absolute-CRUD disclosure (RPD-022, CRS 46), direct-GA/zero-critical-defect gate (`RGL-412`, CRS 43), penetration test (`RGL-402`, CRS 42) — 26 real, cited items scored in full. Foundation blockers (repo strategy, boundaries, schema/migrations, RLS/RBAC, config engine, API/jobs/files, CI/environments, test data, observability, backup/recovery, compliance evidence) dominate the top half of the ranking. Risk-adjusted (not phase-reordering) recommendation: pull the `FIN-195`/`HRT-282` external SME *engagement* forward into Phase 0/1 (their capability-prompt WBS position stays at Phase 4/7) since SME review needs only reviewable policy content, not built schema, and external-party lead time is the least controllable variable in the plan. Concurrency lanes: Phase 5/6 fork; four independent Phase-0 tooling ADRs; SME engagement parallel to Phase 0–3 build; design-system foundation parallel to schema/RLS foundation. Risk burn-down plan and 5 recalculation triggers (ADR resolution, runtime facts, estimate change, failure, requirement change) bind this document to re-derivation, not hand-patching. **No new ADR candidate raised; no product decision reopened; no implementation task created or started.**

**Acceptance and closure:** `docs/architecture/15_RISK_RANKED_CRITICAL_PATH.md` §15 exit gates — every critical-path/risk-table item cites a real ID already registered in `13_*.md`/`14_*.md` (spot-checked, none invented), ranking is reproducible (component scores + arithmetic shown per row), uncertainty is explicit (dimension 8 scored per-row, all assumptions separately labeled in §13), no unverified date is claimed anywhere, all four accepted risks (RPD-022, RPD-031/034/036/037, RPD-038) plus both SME gates visibly affect a concrete sequencing/gate mechanism (§7), not just narrative mention. Final status `VERIFIED`. Next eligible task: `CG-S3-ARCH-016` — Step 3 Closure Verification (Prompt 51, the final Step 3 output).

## 6. Ledger maintenance rules

1. Update the active index and detailed record in the same checkpoint.
2. Never delete failed/rolled-back records; preserve recovery evidence.
3. Link decisions, errors, issues, changes, and build logs by stable IDs.
4. Do not mark `READY` from optimism; entry dependencies must be evidenced.
5. Reconcile status with `CARGOGRID_BUILD_STATUS.md` after every transition.
6. A task without documentation and last-known-good checkpoint cannot be `COMPLETED`.
